The table counts supported stock-days, qualifying first candles (C1), confirmed two-candle setups and actual triggered entries, under our reading of the free lesson and rules page; the indicator itself is behind an email sign-up we did not use, and it may draw its levels differently. [INFERRED] Reading a fortnight as 10 trading days, 7,906 supported stock-days * (1 setup / 10 stock-days) = 790.6 setups, or about 790, versus the counted 32/7,906 supported (7,906/8,685 scheduled), under our reading of the free lesson and rules page; the indicator itself is behind an email sign-up we did not use, and it may draw its levels differently (S:35; J:/Q1/330). Among the registered weekly, fortnightly and twice-weekly readings, once per ten trading days is the least frequent and therefore the smallest, most charitable exposure translation, under our reading of the free lesson and rules page; the indicator itself is behind an email sign-up we did not use, and it may draw its levels differently (P:50). Calendar holidays and the source's "roughly" still make that conversion approximate. Neither 790.6 nor the supported-day gap is a prediction, sampling test, or estimate of what the gated indicator would have produced; do not extend that gap to the full calendar or treat unknown days as zero or as setups. [N:53]

Confirmed two-candle setups are our chosen comparator for "sets up"; C1 is the over-inclusive pre-confirmation alternative, and triggers are realised entries in the replay, under our reading of the free lesson and rules page; the indicator itself is behind an email sign-up we did not use, and it may draw its levels differently (P:50,106; V:528-541 calls Uber a setup before entry, while V:351-356 calls the trigger final confirmation). All three supported counts - C1 89/7,906, confirmed 32/7,906 and triggers 21/7,906 - are below the 790.6 exposure translation on 7,906/8,685 scheduled stock-days, under our reading of the free lesson and rules page; the indicator itself is behind an email sign-up we did not use, and it may draw its levels differently (J:/Q1/330); this observation resolves neither the unknown days nor the full calendar. 3. Taught C0-C5 and fixed retrospective eras, both units/size/costs

The primary reading fades a strong first 15-minute candle at a respected level, requires the next candle to reverse direction without breaking that level, and enters on the silent candle's extreme during the third candle. The preceding regular session supplies the range; earlier completed bars supply outer levels. Numerical strength/tolerance and bounded history are our choices (P:102-106). The stop is beyond the level and formation by one cent, the target is the nearer opposite range level, and an open position flattens at the 15:59 opening print (P:112-120). The narrated word "limit" is retained in the source ledger; implemented touch-through entry uses stop-order mechanics (C:26; P:106). [N:92]

R expresses a trade's result as a fraction of planned dollar risk: net dollars / (100 shares * the distance between order price and stop before entry). +1R means a gain equal to planned risk; -1R means a loss of that size. R is neither account capital nor a loss cap. We sum each trade's own R, not pooled dollars divided by a common risk amount (P:112,122,128). No risk-sized strategy or compounded account was run. [N:94]

Primary C2 has 21/89 fills per C1 opportunity: 10/21 winners, 11/21 losses, 0/21 ties (T:5955). C0 is the idealized zero-expense diagnostic. C1-C5 are estimated expense overlays, not broker quotes or observed costs. Execution expenses are round trip per share; standard short borrow is an assumed 3% annual rate charged for one trading day using /252, without verified availability (P:134-145). [N:96]

R sums each trade's result / own planned risk. Not account capital, a loss cap, compounded equity or a fixed-risk sizing test.

4. Removed trades, same-entry design, R-based label and opposite signs

At 10:00, A requires the silent-candle predicate and B ignores only that predicate. Both use the same entry trigger, formation stop, target, quantity, expiry and validity rules. When both enter, the trades coincide. A removed trade is B filled while A did not; it is not a trade Carl took. This comparison counts predicate exclusions, not the benefit of waiting from 09:45 (P:126-130). [N:144]

[INFERRED] A wide stop puts more dollars behind each R than a narrow stop at fixed share size. The large-dollar NVDA win contributes less than one R, while the small-dollar PFE loss costs more than one R after expenses. Adding dollars weights planned risks differently from adding each trade's own R. That explains the opposite signs; no new position-sizing strategy was tested. SIZING-DEPENDENT, too few to settle. accounting and costsR expresses a trade's result as a fraction of planned dollar risk: net dollars / (100 shares * the distance between order price and stop before entry). +1R means a gain equal to planned risk; -1R means a loss of that size. R is neither account capital nor a loss cap. We sum each trade's own R, not pooled dollars divided by a common risk amount (P:112,122,128). No risk-sized strategy or compounded account was run. 8. Source/figure downloads, exclusions, source limitations and reopen evidence

Downloads and exact scope

  • a31-locked-design.json — Main terms of the private plan, not the full text
  • a31-every-film-figure.csv — Accepted-cut spoken sentences, drawn placements and raster figures with evidence and context
  • a31-trades.csv — Saved opportunity records: both same-entry arms, C0/C2 point accounting; nonentries retained
  • a31-sources.csv — Semantic source IDs, hashes and record meanings
  • a31-library-card.html — Report Library entry

The film inventory contains 136 spoken sentences, 369 distinct drawn-text placements covering 1264 appearances, and 5 raster rows. It covers 50 slides / 152 states. Repeated text at another placement remains a separate row; repeated states at the same placement are listed together. Mascot-only cutaways carry no figure. Source-video timestamps and chart labels are attribution or source text, not our performance measurements.

Film CSV columns: figure_id is a unique inventory key; medium separates spoken sentences, drawn text and raster transcriptions; location names the beat/sentence, slide/role/states, or actual encoded frame. words preserves literal text (empty for a raster with no legible text; its context says so); evidence_contexts names its interpretation; source_ids gives exact cited coordinates; provenance supplies source and inference limits; context preserves the enclosing narration or board. A repeated chart raster is grouped with its other placements, explicitly named in context. The table below displays four combined columns; the download retains all eight.

Opportunity ledger — the displayed columns

DESCRIPTIVE SIMULATION; 100 SHARES PER TRADE. C0: idealized zero expenses. C2: estimated $0.04/share round trip plus assumed 3% annual short borrow /252 for one trading day; availability unverified. R sums each trade's result / own planned risk. Not account capital, a loss cap, compounded equity or a fixed-risk sizing test.

One row represents one saved first-candle opportunity in one registered reading. The 1,889 records span all 22 readings; their results must not be added across designs. A is the taught arm; B drops only the silent-candle predicate at the same entry. Filled=0 is a nonentry, not a tied executed trade. The export retains source precision for C0 and C2 R/dollar point results and planned dollar risk, at 100 shares. A SIZING-DEPENDENT label means at least one displayed R/dollar pair has opposite signs. [N:144; N:210; N:242; P:126-128]

Ledger columns: row is the physical saved-record line; date and stock identify the session; design identifies the registered reading; direction is long or short; shares is quantity; confirmed is the silent-candle flag. A_filled and B_filled identify executed versus nonentered arms. Each arm’s planned_risk_dollars is its pre-entry planned dollar risk. C0_R, C0_dollars, C2_R and C2_dollars are saved point outcomes under the named expense cell, separately for each arm. status repeats the descriptive, size and cost conditions; sizing flags opposite signs within a displayed pair; source is the saved-record coordinate. For nonentries, stored zero result fields do not represent an executed flat trade. The download has 22 columns; the table omits the repeated status column because the same conditions appear immediately above it.
